\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathfrak{c}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}(\underline{x},\underline{x}^{\prime})\leq\frac{1}{2\tau}(2L+\sqrt{2\tau P})^{2}+3P
.

Proof.

Using
Remark

18
, we see that

(5.15)

\frac{1}{2\tau}d(x_{0},x_{1})^{2}\leq E_{t}(x_{0})-E_{t,\tau}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(x_{0})+\frac{1}{2\tau}d(x_{0},x_{1})^{2}=\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}(\underline{x})\leq P,

Combining this with
d(x_{0},x^{\prime}_{0})\leq 2d_{\mathscr{P}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}}(\underline{x},\underline{x}^{\prime})\leq 2L
, which follows directly from the definition of
d_{\mathscr{P}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}}
in
Equation

4.19
,
we see —using the triangular inequality— that
d(x_{1},x^{\prime}_{0})\leq 2L+\sqrt{2\tau P}
.
Furthermore, we have for
\underline{x}^{\prime\prime}\coloneqq(x_{1},x^{\prime}_{0})
, that

\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}(\underline{x}^{\prime\prime})\leq\frac{1}{2\tau}d(x_{1},x^{\prime}_{0})^{2}+P
, since
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}(i^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(x_{1}))\leq P
.
The lemma now follows by considering as a competitor
for
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathfrak{c}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}
the curve
\gamma\colon[1,3]_{\mathbb{Z}}\to\mathscr{P}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}
defined as
\gamma(1)\coloneqq\underline{x}
,
\gamma(2)\coloneqq\underline{x}^{\prime\prime}
,
\gamma(3)\coloneqq\underline{x}^{\prime}
and noting that, owing to the hypotheses,

\sum_{s=2}^{3}\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}(\gamma(s))\leq\frac{1}{2\tau}(2L+\sqrt{2\tau P})^{2}+3P
.
∎

Lemma 5.10
.

Let us assume
1
,
2
,
3
and
4
.
For all
K\subseteq\mathscr{P}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}
compact and
C>0
,
there exists
K^{\prime}\subseteq\mathscr{P}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}
compact such that the following implication holds
for all
\underline{x},\underline{x}^{\prime}\in K
and
\gamma\in\Gamma(\underline{x},\underline{x}^{\prime})
:

(5.16)

\sum_{s=a+1}^{b}\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}(\gamma(s))\leq C\implies\im(\gamma)\subseteq K^{\prime}.

In particular, for each
K\subseteq\mathscr{P}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}
there exists a compact set
K\subseteq\mathscr{P}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}
such that for every
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
the following implication holds
for all
\underline{x},\underline{x}^{\prime}\in K
and
\gamma\in\Gamma(\underline{x},\underline{x}^{\prime})
:

(5.17)

\sum_{s=a+1}^{b}\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}(\gamma(s))\leq\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathfrak{c}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}(\underline{x},\underline{x}^{\prime})+\varepsilon\implies\im(\gamma)\subseteq K^{\prime}.

Proof.

We start with (
5.16
) and fix
K\subseteq\mathscr{P}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}
and
C>0
.
Since
\im(\gamma_{1}|_{[a,b-1]})=\im(\gamma_{0}|_{[a+1,b]})
for all
\gamma\in\Gamma(\underline{x},\underline{x}^{\prime})
,
it suffices to show that
\im(\gamma_{1})
is compact in
\mathbb{X}
.
To proceed, we use the coercivity of
E_{t}
(cf.
4
) and
Equation

5.10

to get the following chain of inequalities, for any
s^{\star}\in[a+1,b-1]_{\mathbb{Z}}
:

\displaystyle C

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{s=a+1}^{b}\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}\big(\gamma(s)\big)\geq\sum_{s=s^{\star}+1}^{b}\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}\big(\gamma(s)\big)\geq E_{t}\big(\gamma_{1}(s^{\star})\big)-E_{t}(x^{\prime}_{1})

\displaystyle\geq E_{t}\big(\gamma_{1}(s^{\star})\big)-\max_{(y_{0},y_{1})\in K}E_{t}(y_{1}).

To show (
5.17
), we infer from
Lemma

5.9
,
from the continuity of
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}
—see
Remark

18
— and from the compactness of
K
that

\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathfrak{c}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}
is bounded on
K\times K
by some
C^{\prime}
. Applying (
5.16
) with
C=C^{\prime}+1
finishes the proof.
∎

We are now in a position to prove that the map
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}\colon\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}\to[0,\infty)
is an action.

Lemma 5.11
.

Let us assume
1
,
2
,
3
and
4
. Then,
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}
is an action according to
Definition

2
.

Proof.

We start by choosing an arbitrary compact subset

K\in\mathbb{X}
. It suffices to show that
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}
is lower semicontinuous on
K\times K
.
To this end, we show the continuity of
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\underline{c}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}\colon\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}\to[0,\infty)
on
K
, which is defined as follows:

\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\underline{c}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}(x,x^{\prime})\coloneqq\inf\left\{\sum_{s=a+1}^{b}\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}\big(\gamma(s)\big)\,\middle|\,\gamma\in\underline{\Gamma}\big(i^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(x),i^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(x^{\prime})\big)\right\},

where
\underline{\Gamma}(p,p^{\prime})=\{\gamma\in\Gamma(p,p^{\prime})\mid\dom(\gamma)=[a,b]_{\mathbb{Z}},a<b-1\}
.
Beside the condition
a<b-1
, the definition of
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\underline{c}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}
coincides with the definition of
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}
in (
4.17
).
The continuity of
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\underline{c}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}
on
K\times K
implies the lower semicontinuity of
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}
on
K\times K
.
Indeed, this follows from the facts that (1.)
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\underline{c}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}\geq\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}
and (2.) the set of points where
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\underline{c}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}\neq\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}
is the diagonal in
K\times K
, which is closed
—and on this diagonal,
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}(x,x)=\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}(x,x)
is continuous.

For any
a<b-1\in\mathbb{Z}
,
x,x^{\prime}\in K
and
f:[a+1,b-1]_{\mathbb{Z}}\to\mathbb{X}
, we set

F(f,x,x^{\prime})\colon{[a,b]_{\mathbb{Z}}\to\mathscr{P}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}}
to be the finite sequence

(5.18)

\bigg(i^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(x),\big(x,f(a+1)\big),\big(f(a+1),f(a+2)\big),\dots,\big(f(b),x^{\prime}\big),i^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(x^{\prime})\bigg).

Note here that we allow the case
a=b-2
, in which case the above display should be read as

\big(i^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(x),(x,x^{\prime}),i^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(x^{\prime})\big)
.
Setting
D\coloneqq\{f\colon[a+1,b-1]_{\mathbb{Z}}\to\mathbb{X}\mid a<b-1\}
,
we observe that
F(\cdot,x,x^{\prime})
is a bijection from
D
to
\underline{\Gamma}\big(i^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(x),i^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(x^{\prime})\big)
.
Thus, we can write
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\underline{c}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}
as the infimum over such extensions:

(5.19)

\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\underline{c}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}(x,x^{\prime})=\inf\bigg\{\sum_{s=a+1}^{b}\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}\big(F(f,x,x^{\prime})\big)\,\bigg|\,f\in D,\dom(f)=[a+1,b-1]_{\mathbb{Z}}\bigg\}.

Furthermore, by the virtue of
Lemma

5.10
, we can take the infimum
over only those
f
for which
\im(f)\subseteq K^{\prime}
for some compact
K^{\prime}\subseteq\mathscr{P}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}
.
Setting
D^{\prime}\coloneqq\{f\colon[a+1,b-1]_{\mathbb{Z}}\to K^{\prime}\mid a<b-1\}
and defining the function

\mathfrak{C}\colon D^{\prime}\times K\times K\to\mathbb{R}
by

\mathfrak{C}((f,x,x))\coloneqq\sum_{s\in\dom\big(F(f,x,x)\big)}\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}\big(F(f,x,x^{\prime})(s)\big).

We can thus rewrite
Equation

5.19
as

(5.20)

\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\underline{c}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}(x,x^{\prime})=\inf_{f\in D^{\prime}}\big\{\mathfrak{C}(f,x,x^{\prime})\big\}.

For each
f\in D^{\prime}
,
\mathfrak{C}(f,\cdot,\cdot)
is continuous on
K\times K
,
due to the continuity of
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}
—see
Remark

18
.
If we can show that this continuity is uniform over
f\in D^{\prime}
,
then
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\underline{c}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}
is continuous on
K\times K
, as it is the infimum over equicontinuous functions.
To this end, we pick

x,x^{\prime},\overline{x},\overline{x}^{\prime}\in K
,
f\in D^{\prime}
with
\dom(f)=[a+1,b-1]_{\mathbb{Z}}
and use the definition of
F
in
Equation

5.18
and write

\displaystyle\mathfrak{C}(f,x,x^{\prime})-\mathfrak{C}(f,\overline{x},\overline{x}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg(\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}\big(i^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(x)\big)-\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}\big(i^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(\overline{x})\big)\bigg)+\bigg(\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}\big(\big(x,f(a+1)\big)\big)-\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}\big(\big(\overline{x},f(a+1)\big)\big)\bigg)

\displaystyle+\bigg(\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}\big(\big(f(b),x^{\prime}\big)\big)-\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}\big(\big(f(b),\overline{x}^{\prime}\big)\big)\bigg)+\bigg(\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}\big(i^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(x^{\prime})\big)-\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}\big(i^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(\overline{x}^{\prime}\big))\bigg).

All the above terms go to zero as
(x,x^{\prime})\to(\overline{x},\overline{x}^{\prime})
,

uniformly
over
f(a+1),f(b)\in K^{\prime}
, which shows the claimed equicontinuity.
∎

Our next goal is to show that the action
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}
is generated by curves.
To this end,
we again need to show two intermediate results.

Lemma 5.12
.

Let us assume
1
,
2
,
3
and
4
, and let
\underline{x},\underline{x}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{P}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}
such that there exists a compact connected set
U\subseteq\mathscr{P}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}
for which

dim_{H}(U)<2
,
\{\underline{x},\underline{x}^{\prime}\}\subseteq U
and
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}|_{U}\equiv 0
.
Then
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathfrak{c}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}(\underline{x},\underline{x}^{\prime})=0
.

Proof.

Since
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}|_{U}\equiv 0
implies that
U\subseteq\im(i)
, we can apply
Lemma

5.1

with
\alpha=2
by using
Lemma

5.9
.
∎

Lemma 5.13
.

Let us assume
1
,
2
,
3
,
4
and
7
,
and let
x,x^{\star}\in\mathbb{X}
and
R>0
such that
\{x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}\mid E_{t}(x^{\prime})\leq E_{t}(x)\}\subseteq B_{\frac{R}{4}}(x^{\star})
.
Then, we have that

|\partial E_{t}|(x)=0\iff\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}(i^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(x))=0.

Proof.

We start proving the implication
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}(i^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(x))\implies|\partial E_{t}|(x)=0
.
If
|\partial E_{t}|(x)>0
, then there
exists a sequence
(x^{\prime}_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\to x
such that

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\frac{E_{t}(x^{\prime}_{n})-E_{t}(x)}{d(x^{\prime}_{n},x)}>0
. We thus pick
\varepsilon>0
and a subsequence without relabeling such that

\frac{E_{t}(x^{\prime}_{n})-E_{t}(x)}{d(x^{\prime}_{n},x)}\geq\varepsilon
for all
n
.
For such a sequence, we have, for
n
large enough, that

\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}\big(i^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(x)\big)\geq E_{t}(x)-E_{t}(x^{\prime}_{n})-\frac{d(x,x^{\prime}_{n})^{2}}{2\tau}\geq d(x,x^{\prime}_{n})\left(\varepsilon-\frac{d(x,x^{\prime}_{n})}{2\tau}\right)>0.

For the opposite implication, we assume that
|\partial E_{t}|(x)=0
.
Then, for all
y\neq x
for which
E_{t}(y)<E_{t}(x)
, we set
\underline{d}:=d(x,y)
and use
7
to pick some
0<\varepsilon\leq\frac{R}{2}
and some
\varepsilon
-geodesic
\gamma
between
x
and
y

with natural parametrization, i.e.,
such that
\gamma(0)=x
,
\gamma(\underline{d}+\varepsilon)=y
and
|\dot{\gamma}|\equiv 1
a.e.
Since
x,y\in B_{\frac{R}{4}}(x^{\star})
by hypothesis, we note that
\im(\gamma)\subseteq B_{R}(x^{\star})
and observe that by
Remark

1
and
7
,

\displaystyle E_{t}(x)-E_{t}(y)-\frac{d(x,y)^{2}}{2\tau}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\int_{0}^{\underline{d}+\varepsilon}|\partial E_{t}|(\gamma(s))\,\mathrm{d}s\right|-\frac{\underline{d}^{2}}{2\tau}\leq\int_{0}^{\underline{d}+\varepsilon}L\cdot s\,\,\mathrm{d}s-\frac{d^{2}}{2\tau}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}L(\underline{d}+\varepsilon)^{2}-\frac{d^{2}}{2\tau}\leq\frac{d^{2}}{2}\left(L-\frac{1}{\tau}\right)+\varepsilon L\left(\underline{d}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\right)\leq\varepsilon L\left(\underline{d}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\right).

Since
\varepsilon>0
was arbitrary, we have that
E_{t}(x)-E_{t}(y)-\frac{d(x,y)^{2}}{2\tau}\leq 0
.
Since
y
was arbitrary as well, we have shown that
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}(i^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(x))=0
for such
x
.
∎

We are now ready to prove that the action
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}
is generated by curves.

Lemma 5.14
.

Let us assume
1
,
2
,
4
,
3
,
5
and
7
.
Then, for all
\tau\leq\frac{1}{L}
,
the action
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}
is generated by curves as in
Definition

3
.

Proof.

The fact that
i^{{}^{{}_{M}}}
is a closed immersion is immediate, and the continuity of
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}
was shown
in
Remark

18
.
As for the properties,

1
is immediate,

2
results from
Lemma

5.13
,

4
follows directly from
Lemma

5.10

and
5
descends from
Lemma

5.9
.
We will now show the missing
3
.

The assumptions and
Lemma

5.12
imply that

\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}(x,x^{\prime})=0
whenever
i^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(x)
and
i^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(x^{\prime})
lie in the same connected component of
i^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(\mathscr{C})\subseteq\mathscr{P}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}
, where
\mathscr{C}\coloneqq\{x\in\mathbb{X}\mid|\partial E_{t}|(x)=0\}
.
In this case, by
5
we have that
x
and
x^{\prime}
are connected by a curve
\gamma
in
\mathbb{X}
whose image in
\mathscr{P}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}
under
i^{{}^{{}_{M}}}
has Hausdorff dimension
smaller than
2
. Thus, we can apply
Lemma

5.12
.
For the converse, we assume that
i^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(x)
and
i^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(x^{\prime})
are
not
in the same connected component of
i^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(\mathscr{C})
—because
i^{{}^{{}_{M}}}
is an isometry, this is equivalent to
x
and
x^{\prime}
not being in the same connected component of
\mathscr{C}
.
We choose
\varepsilon>0
,
K^{\prime}\subseteq\mathscr{P}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}
compact
such that implication (
5.17
) in
Lemma

5.10
holds for
K=\{i^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(x),i^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(x^{\prime})\}
and define the compact set
\mathcal{K}\coloneqq K^{\prime}_{0}\cup K^{\prime}_{1}
, where
K^{\prime}_{0},K^{\prime}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{X}
are the projections of
K^{\prime}
over
\mathbb{X}
.
We set
\mathscr{C}_{1},\dots,\mathscr{C}_{n}
to be the connected components of
\mathscr{C}
which intersect
\mathcal{K}
.
We furthermore set
r\coloneqq\min_{i\neq j}\min_{y\in\mathscr{C}_{i},y^{\prime}\in\mathscr{C}_{j}}d(y,y^{\prime})
,
which is nonzero by
5
.
If
x^{\prime}\not\in\mathscr{C}
, we immediately see that
c_{t}(x,x^{\prime})\geq\mathcal{L}_{t}\big(i^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(x^{\prime})\big)>0
.
So we may assume, after reordering, that
x^{\prime}\in\mathscr{C}_{1}
and
x\not\in\mathscr{C}_{1}
.
We set
D\coloneqq\mathcal{K}\setminus\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}B_{\frac{r}{4}}(\mathscr{C}_{i})

and
P_{\min}\coloneqq\min_{y\in D}\mathcal{L}_{t}(i^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(y))
, which is positive by compactness of
\mathcal{K}
and continuity of
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}
.

Now pick any
\gamma\in\Gamma\big(i^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(x),i^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(x^{\prime})\big)
such that

(5.21)

\sum_{s=a+1}^{b}\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}(\gamma(s))\leq\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}(x,x^{\prime})+\varepsilon

where
a<b\in\mathbb{Z}
are such that
\dom(\gamma)=[a,b]_{\mathbb{Z}}
.
Let
s^{\star}
be the maximal
s
such that
\gamma_{0}(s)\in B_{\frac{r}{4}}(\mathscr{C}_{1})
.
Then
\gamma_{1}(s^{\star})\not\in B_{\frac{r}{4}}(\mathscr{C}_{1})
, and either
\gamma_{1}(s^{\star})\in D
or
\gamma_{1}(s^{\star})\in B_{\frac{r}{4}}(\mathscr{C}_{i})
for some
i\neq 1
.
In the former case, we have that

\sum_{s=a}^{b}\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}\big(\gamma(s)\big)\geq\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}\big(\gamma(s^{\star})\big)\geq\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}\big(i^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(\gamma_{1}(s^{\star}))\big)\geq P_{\min}.

In the latter case, we have that

\sum_{s=a}^{b}\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}\big(\gamma(s)\big)\geq\frac{1}{2\tau}d\big(\gamma_{0}(s^{\star}),\gamma_{1}(s^{\star})\big)^{2}\geq\frac{r^{2}}{8\tau}.

Since (
5.21
) holds and
\varepsilon>0
is arbitrary, we infer from the last two inequalities that
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}(x_{1},x_{2})\geq\min(\frac{r^{2}}{8\tau},P_{\min})>0
.
∎

To finish this section, we prove
Proposition

4.5
.

Proof of
Proposition

4.5
.

Let
\tau>0
.
The fact that
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}
is an action as in
Definition

2
is the content of
Lemma

5.11
;
the fact that for
|\partial E_{t}|
Lipschitz continuous with Lipschitz constant
L
and
\tau\leq\frac{1}{L}
,

\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}
is generated by curves as in
Definition

3
is the content of the above
Lemma

5.14
.
What is left to prove is that
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\bar{\omega}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}
as defined in
Definition

5
is compatible with
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}
as in
Definition

2
,
i.e., that for all
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}
,
(u_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\in\mathbb{X}

and increasing sequences
(s_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\in\mathbb{N}

such that
u_{0}=x
,
\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}u_{s_{j}}=x^{\prime}
and

(5.22)

u_{j+1}\in\argmin_{y\in\mathbb{X}}\left\{E_{t}(y)+\frac{1}{2\tau}d\big(u_{j},y\big)^{2}\right\}

for all
s\in[0,\infty)_{\mathbb{Z}}
, we have that

E_{t}(x)-E_{t}(x^{\prime})=\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}(x,x^{\prime}).

However, this follows directly by applying
Lemma

5.8
to the finite curves

\big(i(x),(u_{0},u_{1}),\dots,(u_{s_{j}-1},u_{s_{j}}),i^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(u_{s_{j}})\big)\in\Gamma\big(i^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(x),i^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(u_{s_{j}})\big),

using continuity of
E_{t}
and
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}

and noticing that the convergence of
E_{t}(u_{j})
implies that
E_{t}(u_{s_{i}})-E_{t,\tau}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(u_{s_{i}})\rightarrow 0

as
s\rightarrow\infty
.
∎

5.3.
The BDF2 method for the gradient flow

In this section, we will explore the BDF2 discretization of the gradient flow and prove
Proposition

4.6
.
To the best of our knowledge, we derive here for the first time the action related to the BDF2 scheme.
We use freely the notation in
Equations

4.26
,
4.27
,
4.28
,
4.29
and
4.30
.
In the rest of this section we denote the components of a curve
\gamma\colon I\subseteq\mathbb{Z}\to\mathscr{P}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}=\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}\times\mathbb{X}
by
\gamma_{-1}
,
\gamma_{0}
and
\gamma_{1}
, and the components of points
\underline{x},\underline{x}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{P}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}
by
(x_{-1},x_{0},x_{1})\coloneqq\underline{x}
and
(x^{\prime}_{-1},x^{\prime}_{0},x^{\prime}_{1})\coloneqq\underline{x}^{\prime}
. As we described in
Remark

7
, whenever we assume that
7
holds, we also assume that
\tau\leq\frac{1}{L}
, where
L
is the Lipschitz constant mentioned in
7
.
Substituting
Equations

4.26
,
4.27
,
4.28
and
4.29
into
Equation

4.10
and simplifying, we obtain the action
Equation

4.24
.

Remark 19
.

In the definitions concerning the minimizing movement scheme in
Equations

4.22
and
4.18
,

\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}
resembled
E^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t,\tau}
. In contrast, in the definitions in
Equations

4.29
and
4.25
,

\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}
and
E^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t,\tau}
look quite different, with differing fractions involving
\tau
.
Based on what we have seen on the minimizing movement scheme, one might instead expect the following definition instead
of
Equation

4.29
:

(5.23)

\widetilde{\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}}(\underline{x}):=E_{t}(x_{0})-E^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t,\tau}(x_{0},x_{-1})+\frac{1}{\tau}d(x_{0},x_{1})^{2}-\frac{1}{4\tau}d(x_{-1},x_{1})^{2}.

In fact, we could have chosen the definition of
\widetilde{\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}}
in (
5.23
)
in place of
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}
in (
4.29
):
For every
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}
, every
(\gamma\in\Gamma(i^{{}^{{}_{B}}}(x),i^{{}^{{}_{B}}}(x^{\prime}))
with
\gamma\colon[a,b]_{\mathbb{Z}}\to\mathscr{P}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}
, and every
\tau>0
,
we can use the fact that
d\big(\gamma_{0}(s),\gamma_{1}(s)\big)=d\big(\gamma_{-1}(s+1),\gamma_{0}(s+1)\big)
for
s\in[a+1,b-1]_{\mathbb{Z}}
, while
d\big(\gamma_{-1}(a),\gamma_{0}(a)\big)=d\big(\gamma_{0}(a),\gamma_{1}(a)\big)=0
—and likewise for
\gamma(b)
— to see that

(5.24)

\sum_{s=a+1}^{b}\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}(\gamma(s))=\sum_{s=a+1}^{b}\widetilde{\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}}\big(\gamma(s)\big).

On the other hand, our formulation has the advantage that
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}
is nonnegative, as we show in the next lemma.

Lemma 5.15
.

For any
\underline{x}\in\mathscr{P}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}
, we have that

(5.25)

\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}(\underline{x})\geq E_{t}(x_{0})-E^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t,\tau}(x_{0},x_{-1}),

where equality can only be attained if
d(x_{-1},x_{0})=d(x_{0},x_{1})
.
Furthermore, we have that

(5.26)

E_{t}(x_{0})-E^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t,\tau}(x_{0},x_{-1})\geq\frac{1}{4\tau}d(x_{0},x_{-1})^{2}\geq 0.

Proof.

The inequality (
5.25
) follows directly from the definition of
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}
in
Equation

4.29

and the fact that, for
x,x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}\in\mathbb{X}
, setting
\underline{d}_{1}\coloneqq d(x,x^{\prime})

and
\underline{d}_{2}\coloneqq d(x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime})
:

(5.27)

\frac{1}{4}d(x,x^{\prime\prime})^{2}\leq\frac{1}{4}(\underline{d}_{1}+\underline{d}_{2})^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}(\underline{d}_{1}^{2}+\underline{d}_{2}^{2}),

where the first bound comes from the triangular inequality and the second bound
is attained if
\underline{d}_{1}=\underline{d}_{2}
.
We get inequality (
5.26
) using
x_{0}
as a competitor for the infimum in the definition
of
E^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t,\tau}
in
Equation

4.25
.
∎

Using the reformulation
Equation

5.24
, we see that the BDF2 discretization of the gradient flows is indeed compatible with
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}
.

Lemma 5.16
.

For any
\underline{x}
in
\mathscr{P}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}
, we have that

(5.28)

\widetilde{\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}}(\underline{x})\geq E_{t}(x_{0})-E_{t}(x_{1}),

where equality is attained if and only if
\underline{x}=(x_{-1},x_{0},x_{1})
satisfies

(5.29)

x_{1}\in\argmin_{y\in\mathbb{X}}\left\{E_{t}(y)+\frac{1}{\tau}d(y,x_{0})^{2}-\frac{1}{4\tau}d(y,x_{-1})^{2}\right\}.

In particular, for any
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}
, we have that

(5.30)

\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}(x,x^{\prime})\geq E_{t}(x)-E^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t,\tau}(x^{\prime},x^{\prime})\geq E_{t}(x)-E_{t}(x^{\prime}).

Proof.

To show (
5.28
), we start with the following calculation: for every
\underline{x}\in\mathscr{P}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}

\begin{split}\widetilde{\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}}(\underline{x})&=E_{t}(x_{0})-E^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t,\tau}(x_{0},x_{-1})+\frac{1}{\tau}d(x_{1},x_{0})^{2}-\frac{1}{4\tau}d(x_{1},x_{-1})^{2}\\
&=E_{t}(x_{0})-\inf_{y\in\mathbb{X}}\left\{E_{t}(y)+\frac{1}{\tau}d(y,x_{0})^{2}-\frac{1}{4\tau}d(y,x_{-1})^{2}\right\}\\
&\qquad+\frac{1}{\tau}d(x_{1},x_{0})^{2}-\frac{1}{4\tau}d(x_{1},x_{-1})^{2}\\
&\geq E_{t}(x_{0})-E_{t}(x_{1}).\end{split}

In particular, equality is attained if and only if the infimum is achieved at
x_{1}
, i.e., if
\underline{x}
fulfills
Equation

5.29
.
To show
Equation

5.30
, we first recursively apply

Equation

5.28
to any
\gamma\in\Gamma(\underline{x},\underline{x}^{\prime})
for

\underline{x},\underline{x}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{P}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}
to see that

\sum_{s=a+1}^{b}\widetilde{\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}}\big(\gamma(s)\big)\geq E_{t}\big(\gamma_{1}(a)\big)-E_{t}\big(\gamma_{1}(b)\big).

Equation

5.30
then follows from the definition of
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}
,

Equation

5.24
and the fact that
i^{{}^{{}_{B}}}(x_{1})_{1}=x_{1}
and
i^{{}^{{}_{B}}}(x_{2})_{1}=x_{2}
.
∎

Next, we investigate the relation between
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}
and
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}
. To avoid confusion, we denote the admissible curves for the BDF2 method, defined in
Equation

4.28
, by
\Gamma^{{}^{{}_{B}}}
,
and we denote the admissible curves for the minimizing movement scheme, defined in
Equation

4.21
, by
\Gamma^{{}^{{}_{M}}}
.
We start by defining a bijection between certain
sets of admissible curves. For
\underline{x},\underline{x}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{P}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}
, we define
F\colon\Gamma^{{}^{{}_{B}}}(\underline{x},\underline{x}^{\prime})\to\Gamma^{{}^{{}_{M}}}\big((x_{0},x_{1}),(x^{\prime}_{-1},x^{\prime}_{0})\big)
as follows:

(5.31)

F(\gamma)\coloneqq\bigg(\big(\gamma_{0}(a),\gamma_{1}(a)\big),\dots,\big(\gamma_{0}(b-1),\gamma_{1}(b-1)\big)\bigg),

where we choose the domain of
F(\gamma)
to be
[a,b-1]_{\mathbb{Z}}
and where
a<b\in\mathbb{Z}
are chosen such that
\dom(\gamma)=[a,b]_{\mathbb{Z}}
.
From the definitions of
\Gamma^{{}^{{}_{B}}}
and
\Gamma^{{}^{{}_{M}}}
, both injectivity and surjectivity of
F
follow right away.

Lemma 5.17
.

For every
\tau>0
, every
\underline{x},\underline{x}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{P}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}
, and every
\gamma\in\Gamma^{{}^{{}_{B}}}(\underline{x},\underline{x}^{\prime})
we have that

(5.32)

\frac{1}{5}\sum_{s=a+1}^{b-1}\,\,\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\frac{\tau}{2}}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}(F(\gamma)(s))\leq\sum_{s=a+1}^{b}\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}(\gamma(s))\leq 3\sum_{s=a+1}^{b-1}\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}(F(\gamma)(s))+\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}\big(\gamma(a)\big).

In particular, for
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}
, we have that

(5.33)

\frac{1}{5}\,\,\,\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\frac{\tau}{2}\,}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}(x,x^{\prime})\leq\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t,\tau}(x,x^{\prime})\leq 3\,\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t,\tau}(x,x^{\prime})+\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}(i^{{}^{{}_{B}}}(x)).

Furthermore, for every
x\in\mathbb{X}
, we have that

(5.34)

\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}(i^{{}^{{}_{B}}}(x))=\,\,\,\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\frac{2\tau}{3}}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}(i^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(x)).

Proof.

For the first inequality, let
\gamma\in\Gamma^{{}^{{}_{B}}}(\underline{x},\underline{x}^{\prime})
be such that
\gamma\colon[a,b]_{\mathbb{Z}}\to\mathscr{P}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}
and choose a sequence of quasi-optimal points
(y_{s})_{s=a+1}^{b}\in\mathbb{X}
such that

E_{t}(y_{s})+\frac{1}{\tau}d\big(\gamma_{0}(s),y_{s}\big)^{2}\leq E_{t,\frac{\tau}{2}}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(\gamma_{0}(s))+\frac{\varepsilon}{b-a+1}.

We then have

\begin{split}\sum_{s=a+1}^{b-1}\,\,&\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\frac{\tau}{2}}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}(F(\gamma)(s))-\varepsilon\\
&\leq\sum_{s=a+1}^{b-1}\bigg(E_{t}(\gamma_{0}(s))-\underbrace{\big(E_{t}(y_{s})+\frac{1}{\tau}d(\gamma_{0}(s),y_{s})^{2}\big)}_{\geq E^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t,\tau}(\gamma_{0}(s),\gamma_{-1}(s))}+\frac{1}{\tau}d\big(\gamma_{0}(s),\gamma_{1}(s)\big)^{2}\bigg)\\
&\leq\sum_{s=a+1}^{b-1}\bigg(E_{t}\big(\gamma_{0}(s)\big)-E^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t,\tau}\big(\gamma_{0}(s),\gamma_{-1}(s)\big)\bigg)\\
&\qquad+4\sum_{s=a+1}^{b-1}\underbrace{\frac{1}{4\tau}d\big(\gamma_{-1}(s+1),\gamma_{0}(s+1)\big)^{2}}_{\leq E_{t}\big(\gamma_{0}(s)\big)-E^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t,\tau}\big(\gamma_{0}(s),\gamma_{-1}(s)\big)\text{ by \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lemma:BDF2_estimates}}}\\
&\leq 5\sum_{s=a+1}^{b}\bigg(E_{t}\big(\gamma_{0}(s)\big)-E^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t,\tau}\big(\gamma_{0}(s),\gamma_{-1}(s)\big)\bigg)\leq 5\sum_{s=a+1}^{b}\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}\big(\gamma(s)\big),\end{split}

where we used
Equation

5.25
in the last inequality.
Since
\varepsilon
was arbitrary, the left inequality in (
5.32
) follows.

To prove the right inequality in (
5.32
),
we likewise fix
\gamma\colon[a,b]_{\mathbb{Z}}\to\mathscr{P}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}
,
\gamma\in\Gamma^{{}^{{}_{B}}}(\underline{x},\underline{x}^{\prime})
and choose a sequence of quasi-optimal points
(y_{s})_{s=a+1}^{b}
such that

E_{t,\tau}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}\big(\gamma_{0}(s),\gamma_{-1}(s)\big)+\frac{\varepsilon}{b-a+1}\geq E_{t}(y_{s})+\frac{1}{\tau}d\big(\gamma_{0}(s),y_{s}\big)^{2}-\frac{1}{4\tau}d\big(\gamma_{-1}(s),y_{s}\big)^{2}.

Owing to the estimate in
Equation

5.27
, we find

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \sum_{s=a+1}^{b-1}\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\bar{\mathcal{L}}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}(\gamma)-\varepsilon\vphantom{\frac{1}{4\tau}}\,\\
\vphantom{\frac{1}{4\tau}}\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \leq\sum_{s=a+1}^{b-1}\bigg(&E_{t}\big(\gamma_{0}(s)\big)-E_{t}(y_{s})-\frac{1}{\tau}d\big(\gamma_{0}(s),y_{s}\big)^{2}+\frac{1}{4\tau}d\big(\gamma_{-1}(s),y_{s}\big)^{2}\\
&+\frac{1}{2\tau}\big(\overline{d}_{s}^{2}+\underline{d}_{s}^{2}\big)-\frac{1}{4\tau}\overline{\underline{d}}_{s}^{2}\bigg)\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\leq\sum_{s=a+1}^{b-1}\Bigg(E_{t}\big(\gamma_{0}(s)\big)-\underbrace{\big(E_{t}(y_{s})+\frac{1}{2\tau}d(\gamma_{0}(s),y_{s})^{2}\big)}_{\geq E_{t,\tau}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(\gamma_{0}(s))}+\frac{1}{2\tau}\underline{d}_{s}^{2}+\frac{1}{2\tau}\big(\overline{d}_{s}^{2}+\underline{d}_{s}^{2}\big)\Bigg)

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\leq\sum_{s=a}^{b-1}\left(E_{t}\big(\gamma_{-1}(s)\big)-E_{t,\tau}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}\big(\gamma_{0}(s)\big)+3\frac{1}{2\tau}\overline{d}^{2}\right)\leq 3\sum_{s=a}^{b-1}\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}\big(F(\gamma)(s)\big),

where we set
\underline{d}_{s}\coloneqq d\big(\gamma_{-1}(s),\gamma_{0}(s)\big)
,
\overline{d}_{s}\coloneqq d\big(\gamma_{0}(s),\gamma_{1}(s)\big)
and

\overline{\underline{d}}_{s}\coloneqq d\big(\gamma_{-1}(s),\gamma_{1}(s)\big)
, and where we used

Equation

5.27
in the second line.
Since
\varepsilon
was arbitrary, the second inequality in (
5.32
) follows.

Equation

5.33
follows directly by applying the infimum to the preceding inequality,
while
Equation

5.34
follows from a simple
substitution into the definitions of
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}
and
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}
in
Equations

4.22
and
4.29
, respectively.
∎

The preceding lemma allows us to adapt some results from the minimizing movement scheme directly.

Lemma 5.18
.

Let us assume
1
,
2
,
3
and
4
.
For all
K\subseteq\mathscr{P}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}
compact and
C>0
,
there exist
K^{\prime}\subseteq\mathscr{P}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}
compact such that the following implication holds
for all
\underline{x},\underline{x}^{\prime}\in K
and
\gamma\in\Gamma(\underline{x},\underline{x}^{\prime})
:

(5.35)

\sum_{s=a+1}^{b}\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}(\gamma(s))\leq C\implies\im(\gamma)\subseteq K^{\prime}.

In particular, for each
K\subseteq\mathscr{P}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}
there exists a compact set
K^{\prime}\subseteq\mathscr{P}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}
such that for every
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
the following implication holds
for all
\underline{x},\underline{x}^{\prime}\in K
and
\gamma\in\Gamma(\underline{x},\underline{x}^{\prime})
:

(5.36)

\sum_{s=a+1}^{b}\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}(\gamma(s))\leq\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathfrak{c}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}(\underline{x},\underline{x}^{\prime})+\varepsilon\implies\im(\gamma)\subseteq K^{\prime}.

Proof.

For the implication in (
5.35
), we use the first inequality in (
5.32
)
and the corresponding implication (
5.16
) in
Lemma

5.10
for
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\frac{\tau}{2}}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}
.
For (
5.36
), we use the second inequality in (
5.33
)
and the continuity of
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}
,
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}
and
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}
to find an upper bound
C^{\prime}
on
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathfrak{c}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}(\underline{x},\underline{x}^{\prime})
for
\underline{x},\underline{x}^{\prime}\in K
,
and we apply (
5.35
) with
C=C^{\prime}+\varepsilon
.
∎

Lemma 5.19
.

Let us assume
1
,
2
,
3
and
4
. Then,
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}
is an action according to
Definition

2
.

Proof.

The proof is analogous to the one for the corresponding result in the minimizing movement scheme,
namely
Lemma

5.11
.
∎

We are now ready to prove that the action
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}
is generated by curves.

Lemma 5.20
.

Let us assume
1
,
2
,
4
,
3
,
5
and
7
.
Then, for all
\tau\leq\frac{1}{L}
,
the action
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}
is generated by curves as in
Definition

3
.

Proof.

First note that by
Lemma

5.15
,
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}
is nonnegative.
For the continuity of
E^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t,\tau}
—and thus of
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}
— we
argue as in
Remark

18
and note that
only points which are either in the set
\{y\in\mathbb{X}\mid d(y,x)\leq d(x,x^{\prime})\}
or in the set
\{y\in\mathbb{X}\mid E_{t}(y)\leq E_{t}(x)\}
contribute to the infimum of

(5.37)

y\mapsto E_{t}(y)+\frac{1}{\tau}d(x,y)^{2}-\frac{1}{4\tau}d(x^{\prime},y)^{2}\geq E_{t}(y)+\frac{1}{\tau}d(x,y)^{2}-\frac{1}{4\tau}\big(d(x,y)+d(x,x^{\prime})\big)^{2}

in
Equation

4.25
: If
y
is in neither set, then the value of (
5.37
)
is large than when using
y=x
as a competitor.
Due to the coercivity of
E_{t}
, both those sets are compact.
Thus,
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}
is itself continous on
\mathscr{P}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}\times[0,T]
, as it is locally a supremum of equicontinous functions.
As for the properties,
1
is immediate
and
4
follows directly from
Lemma

5.18
.
We now prove the remaining three properties.

2
:

We use the first statement in
Lemma

5.15
to see that the assumption

\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}(\underline{x})=0

implies that
E_{t}(x_{0})-E^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t,\tau}(x_{0},x_{-1})=0
and
d(x_{-1},x_{0})=d(x_{0},x_{1})
.
Using the second statement in
Lemma

5.15
, we see that he former implies that
x_{-1}=x_{0}
,
which by the latter implies that also
x_{-1}=x_{0}=x_{1}
and thus that
\underline{x}=i^{{}^{{}_{B}}}(x)
for some
x\in\mathbb{X}
.
By
Equation

5.34
, we then also have that

\,\,\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\frac{2\tau}{3}}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}(i^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(x))=0
, which by the assumptions
\tau\leq\frac{1}{L}

and
Lemma

5.13
implies that
|\partial E_{t}|(x)=0
.

3
:

Note that under the given assumptions, both
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}
and
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\frac{\tau}{2}}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}
are generated by curves
as in
Definition

3
.
Furthermore, in virtue of
Lemma

5.13
and
Equation

5.34
,
under the given assumptions
\tau\leq\frac{1}{L}
, we have that

|\partial E_{t}|(x)=0\iff\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\frac{\tau}{2}}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}(i^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(x))=0\iff\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}(i^{{}^{{}_{B}}}(x))=0\iff\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}(i^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(x))=0.

Using these equivalences, the “if” direction of
3
follows directly from the upper bound by
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}
in (
5.33
) and the respective
3

of
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}
.
The “only if” direction follows from the lower bound by
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\frac{\tau}{2}}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}
in (
5.33
)
and the respective
3

of
\,\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\frac{\tau}{2}}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}
.

5
:

Let
K\subseteq\mathscr{P}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}
be compact and
\varepsilon>0
be chosen. By continuity of
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}
,
we can choose
L^{\prime}
such that for all
\underline{x},\underline{x}^{\prime}\in K
, the following implication holds:

(5.38)

\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}(\underline{x})\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{8},\quad d_{\mathscr{P}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}}(\underline{x},\underline{x}^{\prime})\leq L^{\prime}\implies\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}(\underline{x}^{\prime})\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{4}.

Since
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}
is continuous and vanishes only on the diagonal of
\mathscr{P}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}
,
we can further pick some
\varepsilon^{\prime}\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{8}
such that for all
\underline{x}\in K
,

(5.39)

\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}(\underline{x})\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}\implies\max\big\{d(x_{-1},x_{0}),d(x_{0},x_{1}),d(x_{1},x_{-1})\big\}\leq\frac{L^{\prime}}{2}.

Then, for any
\underline{x},\underline{x}^{\prime}\in K
such that
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}(\underline{x}^{i})\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}
for
i\in\{1,2\}
and
d(\underline{x},\underline{x}^{\prime})\leq\frac{L^{\prime}}{2}
,
we set
\widetilde{\underline{x}}=(x_{0},x_{1},x^{\prime}_{-1})
and
\widetilde{\underline{x}}^{\prime}=(x_{1},x^{\prime}_{-1},x^{\prime}_{0})
.
Then, by
Equation

5.39
and using the triangular inequality,
d_{\mathscr{P}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}}(\widetilde{\underline{x}},\underline{x})\leq L^{\prime}

and
d_{\mathscr{P}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}}(\widetilde{\underline{x}}^{\prime},\underline{x}^{\prime})\leq L^{\prime}
. Thus, using
Equation

5.38
, we have that

\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}(\widetilde{\underline{x}})\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{4}
and
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}(\widetilde{\underline{x}}^{\prime})\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{4}
.
We finish the proof by noting that
(\underline{x},\widetilde{\underline{x}},\widetilde{\underline{x}}^{\prime},\underline{x}^{\prime})\in\Gamma(\underline{x},\underline{x}^{\prime})
.
∎

To finish this section, we proof
Proposition

4.6
.

Proof of
Proposition

4.6
.

Let
\tau>0
.
The fact that
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}
is an action as in
Definition

2
is the content of
Lemma

5.19
;
the fact that for
|\partial E_{t}|

L
-Lipschitz (
L>0
) and
\tau\leq\frac{1}{L}
,

\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}
is generated by curves as in
Definition

3
is the content of the above
Lemma

5.20
.
What is left to prove is that
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\bar{\omega}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}
as defined in
Definition

6
is compatible with
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}
as in
Definition

2
,
i.e., that for all
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}
,
(u_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\in\mathbb{X}
and increasing sequences
(s_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\in\mathbb{N}

such that
u_{0}=x
,
\lim_{j\rightarrow\infty}u_{s_{j}}=x^{\prime}
and

(5.40)

u_{j+1}\in\argmin_{y\in\mathbb{X}}\left\{E_{t}(y)+\frac{1}{\tau}d(y,u_{j})^{2}-\frac{1}{4\tau}d(y,u_{j-1})^{2}\right\},

for all
j\in[0,\infty)_{\mathbb{Z}}
—where we set
u_{-1}=x
—, we have that

E_{t}(x)-E_{t}(x^{\prime})=\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}(x,x^{\prime}).

However, this follows directly by applying recursively applying
Equation

5.28
from
Lemma

5.16
to the sequences

\gamma^{(i)}\coloneqq\big(i(x),(x,x,u_{1}),(x,u_{1},u_{2}),(u_{1},u_{2},u_{3}),\dots,(u_{s_{j}-2},u_{s_{j}-1},u_{s_{j}}),(u_{s_{j}-1},u_{s_{j}},u_{s_{j}}),i(u_{s_{j}})\big)

to see that

\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}(x,u_{s_{j}})\leq E_{t}(x)-E_{t}(u_{s_{j}})+\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}\big((u_{s_{j}-1},u_{s_{j}},u_{s_{j}})\big)+\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}\big(i(u_{s_{j}})\big)

We finish the proof by using continuity of
E_{t}
and
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}

and noting that the convergence of
E_{t}(u_{j})
implies that

\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}\big((u_{s_{j}-1},u_{s_{j}},u_{s_{j}})\big)+\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}\big(i(u_{s_{j}})\big)\rightarrow 0
as
j\rightarrow\infty
.
∎

6.
Numerical experiments

To illustrate our framework, we present an experiment where we simulate the breaking of an elastic rod (
Figure

4
)
2
2

2

Videos of the simulations, as well as the full code are available at
https://github.com/duesenfranz/quasistatic_evolutions_simulations
.
.
The rod is modeled as a chain of
n
particles connected by
n-1
springs;
each of the springs can either be intact or broken.
The total energy of the system is a trade-off of two energies:
On one hand, the potential energy
V
of a spring of length
l
is given as

V(l)=\frac{1}{2}k(l-\bar{l})^{2}
if the spring is intact, and zero otherwise—where
\bar{l}
is the rest length of the spring.
The surface energy
S
of the
i
th spring, on the other hand, is modelled as
S=\sigma_{i}

if the spring is broken, and zero otherwise—here,
\sigma_{i}\sim\mathcal{N}(\bar{\sigma},\varepsilon)
is the surface constant of the
i
th spring, where we add slight noise to break the symmetry.
The total energy of the system is then given by

(6.1)

E(x)=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{B}}\sigma_{i}+\sum_{i\in\mathbb{I}}V(|x_{i}-x_{i+1}|),

where
\mathbb{I}\subseteq[n-1]
is the set of intact springs,
\mathbb{B}=[n-1]\setminus\mathbb{I}
is the set of broken springs, and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
is the vector of the positions of the particles.

Figure 4
.
The simulation for an elastic rod, once for (a)
\delta=\frac{1}{15}
and once for (b)
\delta=\frac{1}{240}
.
From left to right, we plot (i) the initial configuration, (ii) the configuration right before the system transition which leads to the breakage of the rod, (iii) the configuration right after the transition, and (iv) the configuration at the end of the time horizon.
In (a.ii), the stress is concentrated on the two end segments, which leads to the rod breaking early. This phenomenon is the result of the approximation error due to the large step size
\delta=\frac{1}{15}
.

To model the transition between the intact and broken states,
we introduce a latent variable
z\in[0,1]^{n-1}
for each spring
i
.
The variable
z_{i}
is a measure of how broken the
i
th spring is:

z_{i}=0
means that the spring is intact, while
z_{i}=1
means that the spring is completely broken.
With this notation, the total energy of the system becomes

(6.2)

E\big((x,z)\big)=\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}z_{i}\cdot\sigma_{i}+(1-z_{i})\cdot V(|x_{i}-x_{i+1}|).

While
z\in[0,1]^{n-1}
theoretically allows for unphysical states where
z_{i}\not\in\{0,1\}
,
such states will not appear in practice:
In the generic case, we expect that
{V(|x_{i}-x_{i+1}|)\neq\sigma_{i}}
, in which case a critical point must have
z_{i}\in\{0,1\}
.

In the simulation, we fix the first and the last particle to be at position

{x_{1}(t):=(0,-t\cdot h)}
and
x_{n}(t):=(1,t\cdot h)
respectively, at time
t
.
We set the state space to be
E\coloneqq\mathbb{R}^{{n-2}\times 2}\times[0,1]^{[n-1]}
, since each system state is given by the positions of the inner particles
x^{\prime}:=(x_{2},\dots,x_{n-1})\in\mathbb{R}^{n-2\times 2}
as well as the latent variables

z:=z_{1},\dots,z_{n-1}\in[0,1]^{n-1}
. In this setup, the total energy is given by

(6.3)

\displaystyle E_{t}\big((\bar{x},\bar{z})\big)

\displaystyle=\overbrace{(1-z_{1})\cdot V(|x_{2}-(0,-t\cdot h)|)}^{\text{Tension in first segment}}+\overbrace{\sum_{i=2}^{n-2}(1-z_{i})\cdot V(|x_{i+1}-x_{i}|)}^{\text{Tension in inner segments}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{(1-z_{n-1})\cdot V(|x_{n-1}-(1,t\cdot h)|)}_{\text{Tension in last segment}}+\underbrace{\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}z_{i}\cdot\sigma_{i}}_{\text{Surface energy}}.

Finally, we set the starting state to be the local minimum where the whole rod is intact and
the points
x
are equidistantly spaced along the line from
x_{1,0}
to
x_{n,0}
.
The discrete quasistatic evolution follows this local minimum over time,
positioning the points
x
equidistantly between the endpoints
x_{1}(t)
and
x_{n}(t)
.
Eventually, the potential energy of one of the springs exceeds the surface energy.
At this point, this local minimum disappears and the discrete quasistatic evolution jumps to a lower energy level—the one where the spring is broken.
Interestingly, this new local minimum of the rod being broken is not an isolated critical point, but a whole manifold.

Numerically, we use a BDF2 approximation of the gradient flow, corresponding to the action explained in
Section

5.3
, with
\tau=0.1
; we stop the gradient descent once the energy difference between two consecutive steps is less than
10^{-5}
.

We can observe the expected convergences in the quantities involved in the energy balance: First,
\mu^{\delta}
converges to zero everywhere but at a single point—the point where the rod breaks—as
\delta
goes to zero (
Figure

6(a)
). Furthermore,
\mathcal{D}^{\delta}
converges to a piecewise smooth function, with a single jump point where the rod breaks (
Figure

6(b)
).
The same convergences can be seen when comparing
E_{t}(\eta^{\delta}(t))
and
\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}^{\delta}(s)\,\mathrm{d}s
for different values of
\delta
(
Figure

5
).

Figure 5
.
Comparing
E_{t}(\eta^{\delta}(t))
and
\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{D}^{\delta}(s)\,\mathrm{d}s
, for
\delta=\frac{1}{15}
on the left and
\delta=\frac{1}{240}
on the right.

(a)

\mu^{\delta}
over the time horizon.

(b)

\mathcal{D}^{\delta}
over the time horizon.

Figure 6
.
The quantities involved in the energy balanced for the elastic rod simulation, for discrete quasistatic evolutions with different values of
\delta
.
For
\delta
going to zero, the support of
\mu^{\delta}
collapses to the single point where the rod breaks at
t=0.78
. Furthermore,
\mathcal{D}^{\delta}
converges to a piecewise smooth function,
with a single jump point at this breakage time. After the rod is broken,
the potential energy is constant zero for steady states and
D^{\delta}
converges to zero as
\delta
goes to zero.

Appendix A
Structure of the measure in the energy balance

In this part, we seek to establish that the non-negative measure
\bar{\mu}
appearing in the energy balance in
Theorem

1 (complete)
is purely atomic.
Under suitable extra assumptions, this fact is expected, as it has already been observed for quasistatic evolutions in finite-dimensional Hilbert spaces constructed with limiting arguments (see, e.g.,
[
2
,
25
,
21
]
).
In this section, we adapt to our setting the arguments developed in
[
25
, Section 5.1]
.

We observe that, by virtue of
8
, we can rewrite the energy balance for the quasistatic evolution
\hat{\eta}\colon[0,T]\to\mathcal{X}
as follows:

(A.1)

\hat{E}\big(\hat{\eta}^{+}(t)\big)-\hat{E}\big(\hat{\eta}^{-}(s)\big)=\int_{s}^{t}\partial_{t}\hat{E}\big(\hat{\eta}(\tau)\big)\,\mathrm{d}\tau-\bar{\mu}([s,t]).

Moreover, we introduce the non-increasing function
f\colon[0,T]\to\mathbb{R}
as

(A.2)

f(t)\coloneqq\hat{E}\big(\hat{\eta}(t)\big)-\int_{0}^{t}\partial_{t}\hat{E}\big(\hat{\eta}(\tau)\big)\,\mathrm{d}\tau,

and we use the notation
f_{+}(t)\coloneqq\lim_{s\to t^{+}}f(s)
. We observe right away that

f_{+}(t)=\lim_{s\to t^{+}}\left(\hat{E}\big(\hat{\eta}(s)\big)-\int_{0}^{s}\partial_{t}\hat{E}\big(\hat{\eta}(\tau)\big)\,\mathrm{d}\tau\right)=\hat{E}(\hat{\eta}^{+}(t)\big)-\int_{0}^{t}\partial_{t}\hat{E}\big(\hat{\eta}(\tau)\big)\,\mathrm{d}\tau.

Proposition A.1
.

Let us assume
5
,
3
,
1
,
7
,
6
,
2
,
4
and
8
.
Let
f\colon[0,T]\to\mathbb{R}
be defined as in
Equation

A.2
. Then, for every
t\in[0,T)
, we have that

D_{+}f_{+}(t)\coloneqq\liminf_{h\to 0^{+}}\frac{f_{+}(t+h)-f_{+}(t)}{h}\geq 0,

i.e., the Dini lower right derivative of
f_{+}
is non-negative.

Proof.

For every
s\in[0,T]
, let us take
x_{s}\in\mathbb{X}
such that
(s,x_{s})\in\hat{\eta}^{+}(s)
.
Then, we fix
t\in[0,T)
and we observe that

\begin{split}f_{+}(t+h)-f_{+}(t)&=\hat{E}\big(\eta^{+}(t+h)\big)-\hat{E}\big(\eta^{+}(t)\big)-\int_{t}^{t+h}\partial_{t}\hat{E}\big(\hat{\eta}(\tau)\big)\,\mathrm{d}\tau\\
&=E_{t+h}(x_{t+h})-E_{t}(x_{t})-\int_{t}^{t+h}\partial_{t}\hat{E}\big(\hat{\eta}(\tau)\big)\,\mathrm{d}\tau\\
&=E_{t}(x_{t+h})-E_{t}(x_{t})+\int_{t}^{t+h}\left(\partial_{t}E_{\tau}(x_{t+h})-\partial_{t}\hat{E}\big(\hat{\eta}(\tau)\big)\right)\,\mathrm{d}\tau\end{split}

for every
h>0
.
Moreover, the expression in the intergal can be rewritten as follows:

(A.3)

\begin{split}\partial_{t}E_{\tau}(x_{t+h})-\partial_{t}\hat{E}\big(\hat{\eta}(\tau)\big)&=\partial_{t}\hat{E}\big([(\tau,x_{t+h})]\big)-\partial_{t}\hat{E}\big(\hat{\eta}(\tau)\big)\\
&=\left(\partial_{t}\hat{E}\big([(\tau,x_{t+h})]\big)-\partial_{t}\hat{E}\big(\hat{\eta}^{+}(t+h)\big)\right)\\
&\qquad+\left(\partial_{t}\hat{E}\big(\hat{\eta}^{+}(t+h)\big)-\partial_{t}\hat{E}\big(\hat{\eta}^{+}(t)\big)\right)\\
&\qquad+\left(\partial_{t}\hat{E}\big(\hat{\eta}^{+}(t)\big)-\partial_{t}\hat{E}\big(\hat{\eta}(\tau)\big)\right).\end{split}

Since
[(\tau,x_{t+h})]\to_{\mathcal{X}}[(t+h,x_{t+h})]=\hat{\eta}(t+h)
as
\tau\to t+h
, it follows that the time derivative
\partial_{t}\hat{E}\big([(\tau,x_{t+h})]\big)\to\partial_{t}\hat{E}\big(\hat{\eta}^{+}(t+h)\big)
as
\tau\to t+h
, owing to
8
.
Moreover, recalling that
\tau\in[t,t+h]
, that
\hat{\eta}^{+}
is right-continuous and that
\hat{\eta}^{+}(\tau)\to_{\mathcal{X}}\hat{\eta}^{+}(t)
as
\tau\to t^{+}
, by virtue of
Equation

A.3
and
8
, we conclude that

\int_{t}^{t+h}\left(\partial_{t}E_{\tau}(x_{t+h})-\partial_{t}\hat{E}\big(\hat{\eta}(\tau)\big)\right)\,\mathrm{d}\tau=o(h),

yielding the identity

(A.4)

\liminf_{h\to 0^{+}}\frac{f_{+}(t+h)-f_{+}(t)}{h}=\liminf_{h\to 0^{+}}\frac{E_{t}(x_{t+h})-E_{t}(x_{t})}{h}.

Before proceeding, we need to carefully choose the representative
(t,y)
of
\hat{\eta}^{+}(t)
, and to replace
(t,x_{t})
if necessary.
Indeed, let us firs consider
h_{n}\searrow 0
as
n\to\infty
such that

(A.5)

\liminf_{h\to 0^{+}}\frac{E_{t}(x_{t+h})-E_{t}(x_{t})}{h}=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{E_{t}(x_{t+h_{n}})-E_{t}(x_{t})}{h_{n}},

and such that there exits
x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}
for which
d(x_{t+h_{n}},x^{\prime})\to 0
as
n\to\infty
. We recall that it is possible to find such a sequence by virtue of
Remark

15
.
Moreover, since
[(t+h_{n},x_{t+h_{n}})]=\hat{\eta}^{+}(t+h_{n})\to_{\mathcal{X}}\hat{\eta}^{+}(t)
as
n\to\infty
, we deduce that
(t,x^{\prime})\in\hat{\eta}^{+}(t)
.
Hence, since
[(t,x^{\prime})]=\hat{\eta}^{+}(t)=[(t,x_{t})]
, from
Equations

A.4
and
A.5
we obtain that

(A.6)

\liminf_{h\to 0^{+}}\frac{f_{+}(t+h)-f_{+}(t)}{h}=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{E_{t}(x_{t+h_{n}})-E_{t}(x^{\prime})}{h_{n}},

where we used the fact that
E_{t}(x_{t})=E_{t}(x^{\prime})
, ensured by
5
.
Then, we note that if
E_{t}(x_{t+h_{n}})=E_{t}(x^{\prime})
for infinitely many
n\in\mathbb{N}
, then the thesis follows immediately. Therefore, we assume that
E_{t}(x_{t+h_{n}})\neq E_{t}(x^{\prime})
for
n
large enough.
From this fact, since
d(x_{t+h_{n}},x^{\prime})\to 0
as
n\to\infty
and since the connected components of critical points are assumed to be well-separated (see again
5
), it follows that eventually
|\partial E_{t}|(x_{t+h_{n}})\neq 0
.
Hence, we compute

\frac{E_{t}(x_{t+h_{n}})-E_{t}(x^{\prime})}{h_{n}}=\frac{E_{t}(x_{t+h_{n}})-E_{t}(x^{\prime})}{|\partial E_{t}|(x_{t+h_{n}})}\frac{|\partial E_{t}|(x_{t+h_{n}})}{h_{n}}\geq-\varepsilon_{x^{\prime}}\big(d(x_{t+h_{n}},x^{\prime})\big)L,

where we used
6
, together with the fact that
|\partial E_{t+h_{n}}|(x_{t+h_{n}})=0
, due to
[(t+h_{n},x_{t+h_{n}})]=\hat{\eta}^{+}(t+h_{n})
.
Hence, by taking the limit in the last inequality, we finally deduce that

\liminf_{h\to 0^{+}}\frac{f_{+}(t+h)-f_{+}(t)}{h}=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{E_{t}(x_{t+h_{n}})-E_{t}(x^{\prime})}{h_{n}}\geq 0

and we conclude the proof.
∎

We report below a well-known sufficient condition for monotonicity of continuous functions.

Lemma A.2
.

Let
g\colon[a,b]\to\mathbb{R}
be continuous and such that the Dini upper right derivative of
g
satisfies

D^{+}g^{\prime}(t)\coloneqq\limsup_{h\to 0^{+}}\frac{g(t+h)-g(t)}{h}\geq 0

for every
t\in(a,b)
. Then,
g
is non-decreasing on
(a,b)
.

Proof.

See, e.g.,
[
25
, Lemma 5.2]
.
∎

Now, we show that the non-negative measure
\bar{\mu}
appearing in the energy balance in
Theorem

1 (complete)
is purely atomic.
Since we already took care of the peculiarity of our setting in
Proposition

A.1
, we report the the arguments in the next proof follows the lines of
[
25
, Theorem 5.4]
.
We detail the proof for the sake of completeness.

Proposition A.3
.

Let us assume
5
,
3
,
1
,
7
,
6
,
2
,
4
and
8
.
Let
\bar{\mu}
be the non-negative measure appearing in the energy balance in
Theorem

3.9
. Then, we have that
\mathrm{supp}\,\mu=J
, where
J
is the jump set of the limiting trajectory
\hat{\eta}\colon[0,T]\to\mathcal{X}
.

Proof.

We consider the non-increasing function
f\colon[0,T]\to\mathbb{R}
defined as in
Equation

A.2
, and, owing to
Theorem

3.9
and to the enhanced energy balance in
Equation

A.1
, we observe that the distributional derivative of
f
satisfies
\bar{\mu}=-df
, and we recall
\bar{\mu}
is a positive measure.
To see that
\mathrm{supp}\,\bar{\mu}=J
, we introduce the non-increasing function

f^{J}(t)\coloneqq\sum_{s\in[0,t]}\big(f_{+}(s)-f_{-}(s)\big),

and we observe that it is right-continuous. Moreover, the set of dicontinuity points for
f^{J}
is exactly
J
, and the distributional derivative satisfies
d(f^{J})=(df)^{J}
, where
(df)^{J}
denotes the jump part of the measure
df
. Then, we observe that
\bar{\mu}=-df\geq-d(f^{J})\eqqcolon\bar{\mu}^{J}
.
In addition, recalling that
f^{J}(t)-f^{J}(t+h)\geq 0
for every
h\geq 0
, we have that

(A.7)

\liminf_{h\to 0}\frac{(f_{+}-f^{J})(t+h)-(f_{+}-f^{J})(t)}{h}\geq\liminf_{h\to 0}\frac{f_{+}(t+h)-f_{+}(t)}{h}\geq 0.

We further notice that the function
f_{+}-f^{J}
is continuous. Indeed, on the one hand, both
f_{+}
and
f^{J}
are right-continuous.
On the other hand, we have
\lim_{h\to 0^{+}}f_{+}(t-h)=f_{-}(t)
and

\lim_{h\to 0^{+}}f^{J}(t-h)=\sum_{s\in[0,t)}\big(f_{+}(s)-f_{-}(s)\big)=f^{J}(t)-\big(f_{+}(t)-f_{-}(t)\big)

for every
t\in(0,T]
, which combined provide the left-continuity of
f_{+}-f^{J}
.
Hence, by using
Equation

A.7
and
Lemma

A.2
, we deduce that
f_{+}-f^{J}
is non-decreasing.
Therefore, from the inequality
f_{+}(t)-f^{J}(t)\geq f_{+}(0)-f^{J}(0)
, recalling that
f^{J}(t)=-\bar{\mu}^{J}([0,t])
and that
f_{+}(0)-f^{J}(0)=f_{-}(t)
, we obtain that

\hat{E}\big(\hat{\eta}^{+}(t)\big)+\bar{\mu}^{J}([0,t])\geq\hat{E}\big(\hat{\eta}^{-}(0)\big)+\int_{0}^{t}\partial_{t}\hat{E}\big(\hat{\eta}(\tau)\big)\,\mathrm{d}\tau

for every
t\in[0,T]
.
Recalling that
\bar{\mu}^{J},\bar{\mu}
are positive, from the last inequality and from the balance in
Equation

A.1
we deduce that
\bar{\mu}^{J}\geq\bar{\mu}
. Since by construction
\bar{\mu}\geq\bar{\mu}^{J}
, this shows that
\bar{\mu}=\bar{\mu}^{J}
and concludes the proof.
∎

Appendix B
Gradient flow vs. minimizing movement scheme actions

In this section, we relate the actions
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{B}}}_{t}
and
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{M}}}_{t}
, corresponding to the gradient descent and to the minimizing movement scheme, respectively.
From here on, we fix a time
t\in[0,T]
and consequently drop the subscript
t
from the notation:
We concern ourselves with two actions
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{B}}}
and
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{M}}}

and an energy functional
E
on
\mathbb{X}
.
We begin by relating
\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{F}}}
and
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}
, the instantaneous costs of the gradient flow and of the minimizing movement scheme, respectively.

Lemma B.1
.

Let us assume
5
,
3
,
1
,
7
,
6
,
2
and
4
. Furthermore, let us assume that
L<\frac{1}{\tau}
, where
L
is the Lipschitz constant of
|\partial E|
. Then, setting
\varepsilon_{L}\coloneqq L\cdot\tau
, we have that, for every
x\in\mathbb{X}
,

(B.1)

(1-\varepsilon_{L})\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}\big(i(x)\big)\leq\tau\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{F}}}\big(i(x)\big)\leq(1+\varepsilon_{L})\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}\big(i(x)\big).

Proof.

We start with the first inequality in (
B.1
).
To this end, we fix
x\in\mathbb{X}
, pick
x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}
and
\varepsilon>0
, and set
\underline{d}\coloneqq d(x,x^{\prime})
. We use
1
to choose an absolutely continuous curve
\gamma\colon[0,\underline{d}+\varepsilon]\to\mathbb{X}
such that
\dot{\gamma}\equiv 1
,
\gamma(0)=x
and
\gamma(\underline{d}+\varepsilon)=x^{\prime}
.
Since
|\partial E|
is a strong upper gradient—see
Remark

1
—we have

\displaystyle E(x)-E(x^{\prime})-\frac{1}{2\tau}\underline{d}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\underline{d}+\varepsilon}|\partial E|(\gamma(s))\,\mathrm{d}s-\frac{1}{2\tau}\underline{d}^{2}\leq\int_{0}^{\underline{d}+\varepsilon}\big(|\partial E|(x)+Ls\big)\,\mathrm{d}s-\frac{1}{2\tau}\underline{d}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq(\underline{d}+\varepsilon)|\partial E|(x)+\frac{1}{2}L(\underline{d}+\varepsilon)^{2}-\frac{1}{2\tau}\underline{d}^{2}.

Taking
\varepsilon\to 0
, we get

(B.2)

E(x)-E(x^{\prime})-\frac{1}{2\tau}\underline{d}^{2}\leq\underline{d}|\partial E|(x)+L\frac{1}{2}\underline{d}^{2}-\frac{1}{2\tau}\underline{d}^{2}=\underline{d}|\partial E|(x)-\frac{1}{2\tau}(1-\varepsilon_{L})\underline{d}^{2}.

The right hand side above is a quadratic equation whose maximum is attained at
\underline{d}=\tau|\partial E|(x)\frac{1}{(1-\varepsilon_{L})}
; substituting this choice of
\underline{d}
into
Equation

B.2
gives

E(x)-E(x^{\prime})-\frac{1}{2\tau}\underline{d}^{2}\leq{\tau}\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon_{L}}\frac{1}{2}|\partial E|(x)^{2}

As
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}\big(i(x)\big)
is the supremum of the left hand side of the above equation over all
x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}
, we have

\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}\big(i(x)\big)\leq{\tau}\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon_{L}}\frac{1}{2}|\partial E|(x)^{2}={\tau}\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon_{L}}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{F}}}(i(x)),

which finishes the proof of the first inequality.

For the second inequality in (
B.1
), we start by letting
\gamma\colon[0,\infty)\to\mathbb{X}
be a curve of maximum slope such that
\gamma(0)=x
, which in particular implies that the following equation holds for all
s\geq 0
:

(B.3)

E(x)-E\big(\gamma(s)\big)=\int_{0}^{s}|\partial E|\big(\gamma(r)\big)|\dot{\gamma}|\,\mathrm{d}r;

such a curve of maximum slope exists by
Remark

1
and
[
7
, Prop. 2.2.3, Theorem 2.3.3]
.
For
s>0
, we set
l_{s}\coloneqq\int_{0}^{s}|\dot{\gamma}(\sigma)|\,\mathrm{d}\sigma
and we define
l_{\infty}\coloneqq\int_{0}^{\infty}|\dot{\gamma}(\sigma)|\,\mathrm{d}\sigma
.
Note that, as
\gamma
is a curve of maximum slope, in addition to
Equation

B.3
, the following holds for almost all
s>0
:

|\dot{\gamma}|(s)=|\partial E|(\gamma(s))\leq|\partial E|(x)-L\int_{0}^{s}|\dot{\gamma}|(r)\,\mathrm{d}r.

Using Grönwall’s lemma, we deduce from the above that
|\dot{\gamma}|(s)\geq|\partial E|(x)e^{-Ls}
,
and thus that

(B.4)

l_{\infty}\geq\frac{1}{L}|\partial E|(x).

As a next step, we reparametrize
\gamma
to obtain a curve
\widetilde{\gamma}\colon[0,l_{\infty}]\to\mathbb{X}
such that
\widetilde{\gamma}(0)=x
,

\dot{\widetilde{\gamma}}(s)\equiv 1
and such that
Equation

4.14
holds for
\widetilde{\gamma}

in place of
\gamma
.
We calculate, for
s\in[0,l_{\infty}]
,

\displaystyle\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}\big(i(x)\big)

\displaystyle\geq E(x)-E\big(\widetilde{\gamma}(s))-\frac{1}{2\tau}d\big(x,\widetilde{\gamma}(s)\big)^{2}=\int_{0}^{s}|\partial E|\big(\widetilde{\gamma}(r)\big)\,\mathrm{d}r-\frac{1}{2\tau}d\big(x,\widetilde{\gamma}(s)\big)^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\int_{0}^{s}\left(|\partial E|(x)-Lr\right)\,\mathrm{d}r-\frac{1}{2\tau}s^{2}=s|\partial E|(x)-\frac{1}{2}Ls^{2}-\frac{1}{2\tau}s^{2}

\displaystyle=s|\partial E|(x)-\frac{1}{2}\left(L+\frac{1}{\tau}\right)s^{2}.

Evaluating the above at
s^{\star}\coloneqq\frac{|\partial E|(x)}{L+\frac{1}{\tau}}
—justified by
Equation

B.4
— gives

\displaystyle\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}\big(i(x)\big)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}|\partial E|(x)^{2}\tau\frac{1}{1+\varepsilon_{L}}=\tau\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{F}}}\big(i(x)\big)\frac{1}{1+\varepsilon_{L}},

which finishes the proof of the second inequality.
∎

As a next step, we show that we can bound the cost of a single step of the minimizing movement scheme
by the cost of the gradient descent action, as long as step is small enough.

Lemma B.2
.

Let us assume
5
,
3
,
1
,
7
,
6
,
2
and
4
. Furthermore, let us assume that
L<\frac{1}{\tau}
, where
L
is the Lipschitz constant of
|\partial E|
. Then, setting
\varepsilon_{L}\coloneqq L\cdot\tau
, we have, for each
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}
such that
d(x,x^{\prime})\leq\tau\cdot|\partial E|(x)
,

(B.5)

\begin{split}(1-\varepsilon_{L})\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}\big((x,x^{\prime})\big)&\leq 2c^{{}^{{}_{F}}}(x,x^{\prime})+|\partial E|(x)\big(\tau|\partial E|(x)-d(x,x^{\prime})\big)\\
&\leq 2c^{{}^{{}_{F}}}(x,x^{\prime})+2\tau\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{F}}}\big(i(x)\big).\end{split}

In particular, if
d(x,x^{\prime})=\tau|\partial E|(x)
, the above inequality reduces to

(B.6)

(1-\varepsilon_{L})\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}\big((x,x^{\prime})\big)\leq 2c^{{}^{{}_{F}}}(x,x^{\prime}).

Proof.

Both the second inequality in (
B.5
) and (
B.6
) follow immediatly. In the rest of this proof, we concern ourselves the first inequality in (
B.5
).
Under the above assumptions, we have, using
Lemma

B.1
:

(B.7)

\begin{split}(1-\varepsilon_{L})\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}\big((x,x^{\prime})\big)&=(1-\varepsilon_{L})\bigg(\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}\big(i(x)\big)+\frac{1}{2\tau}d(x,x^{\prime})^{2}\bigg)\\
&\leq\tau\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{F}}}\big(i(x)\big)+\frac{1}{2\tau}\tau^{2}|\partial E|(x)^{2}=2\tau\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{F}}}\big(i(x)\big).\end{split}

On the other hand, we can bound
c^{{}^{{}_{F}}}(x,x^{\prime})
from below: By virtue of
Lemma

5.2
it suffices to find a lower bound for
\int_{0}^{s}|\partial E|(\gamma(\sigma))\,\mathrm{d}\sigma
, uniformly over
s>0
and all absolutely continuous curves
\gamma\colon[0,s]\to\mathbb{X}
such that
\dot{\gamma}\equiv 1
,
\gamma(0)=x
and
\gamma(s)=x^{\prime}
. Let such
s
and
\gamma
be given. We note that
s\geq d(x,x^{\prime})\eqqcolon\underline{d}
and write

\begin{split}\int_{0}^{s}|\partial E|(\gamma(\sigma))\,\mathrm{d}\sigma&\geq\int_{0}^{\underline{d}}|\partial E|(\gamma(\sigma))\,\mathrm{d}\sigma\geq\underline{d}|\partial E|(x)-\frac{1}{2}L\underline{d}^{2}\\
&\geq\frac{1}{2}|\partial E|(x)\underline{d}=\frac{1}{2}\tau|\partial E|(x)^{2}-\frac{1}{2}|\partial E|(x)\big(\tau|\partial E|(x)-\underline{d}\big).\end{split}

Taking the infimum over
\gamma
in the inequality above, we obtain

(B.8)

c^{{}^{{}_{F}}}(x,x^{\prime})\geq\tau\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{F}}}\big(i(x)\big)-\frac{1}{2}|\partial E|(x)\big(\tau|\partial E|(x)-\underline{d}\big).

Combining
Equations

B.7
and
B.8
gives

\displaystyle(1-\varepsilon_{L})\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}\big((x,x^{\prime})\big)

\displaystyle\leq 2c^{{}^{{}_{F}}}(x,x^{\prime})+|\partial E|(x)\big(\tau|\partial E|(x)-\underline{d}\big)

as required.
∎

We continue by proving a inverse relation to
Lemma

B.2
:

Lemma B.3
.

Let us assume
5
,
3
,
1
,
7
,
6
,
2
and
4
. Furthermore, let us assume that
L<\frac{1}{\tau}
, where
L
is the Lipschitz constant of
|\partial E|
. Then, setting
\varepsilon_{L}\coloneqq L\cdot\tau
, we have, for every
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}
:

c^{{}^{{}_{F}}}(x,x^{\prime})\leq 4\frac{1+\varepsilon_{L}}{1-\varepsilon_{L}}\,\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}\big((x,x^{\prime})\big)

Proof.

The proof is similar to the first part of the proof of
Lemma

B.1
.
We show the inequality by choosing, for an arbitrary
\varepsilon>0
, as a competitor for the infimum in
Equation

5.3

an absolutely continuous curve
\gamma\colon[0,\underline{d}+\varepsilon]\to\mathbb{X}
such that
\dot{\gamma}\equiv 1
,
\gamma(0)=x
and
\gamma(\underline{d}+\varepsilon)=x^{\prime}
; the existence of such a curve is guaranteed by
1
.
With this choice, we have, setting
\underline{d}\coloneqq d(x,x^{\prime})
:

c^{{}^{{}_{F}}}(x,x^{\prime})\leq\int_{0}^{\underline{d}+\varepsilon}|\partial E|\big(\gamma(s)\big)\,\mathrm{d}s\leq(\underline{d}+\varepsilon)|\partial E|(x)+\frac{1}{2}L(\underline{d}+\varepsilon)^{2}.

Taking the limit
\varepsilon\to 0
, we get

(B.9)

c^{{}^{{}_{F}}}(x,x^{\prime})\leq\underline{d}|\partial E|(x)+\frac{1}{2}L\underline{d}^{2}=\underline{d}|\partial E|(x)+\frac{1}{2\tau}\underline{d}^{2}-\frac{1}{2\tau}(1-\varepsilon_{L})\underline{d}^{2}.

If
\underline{d}\leq\frac{2\tau}{1-\varepsilon_{L}}|\partial E|(x)
, we use
Lemma

B.1
to reduce the above to

\displaystyle c^{{}^{{}_{F}}}(x,x^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\tau}{1-\varepsilon_{L}}|\partial E|^{2}(x)+\frac{1}{2\tau}\underline{d}^{2}=\frac{4}{1-\varepsilon_{L}}\tau\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{F}}}(i(x))+\frac{1}{2\tau}\underline{d}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 4\frac{1+\varepsilon_{L}}{1-\varepsilon_{L}}\,\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}\big(i(x)\big)+\frac{1}{2\tau}\underline{d}^{2}\leq 4\frac{1+\varepsilon_{L}}{1-\varepsilon_{L}}\,\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}\big((x,x^{\prime})\big).

On the other hand, if
\underline{d}>\frac{2\tau}{1-\varepsilon_{L}}|\partial E|(x)
,

Equation

B.9
also gives

\displaystyle c^{{}^{{}_{F}}}(x,x^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq\underline{d}|\partial E|(x)+\frac{1}{2\tau}\underline{d}^{2}-\frac{1}{2\tau}(1-\varepsilon_{L})\frac{2\tau}{1-\varepsilon_{L}}|\partial E|(x)\underline{d}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\tau}\underline{d}^{2}\leq\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}\big((x,x^{\prime})\big)\leq 4\frac{1+\varepsilon_{L}}{1-\varepsilon_{L}}\,\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}\big((x,x^{\prime})\big).

∎

We are now in position to prove a relation between the actions
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{B}}}
and
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{M}}}
.

Lemma B.4
.

Let us assume
5
,
3
,
1
,
7
,
6
,
2
and
4
. Furthermore, let us assume that
L<\frac{1}{\tau}
, where
L
is the Lipschitz constant of
|\partial E|
. Then, setting
\varepsilon_{L}\coloneqq L\cdot\tau
, we have, for any
x\neq x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{X}

(B.10)

\frac{1-\varepsilon_{L}}{4(1+\varepsilon_{L})}c^{{}^{{}_{F}}}(x,x^{\prime})\leq\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(x,x^{\prime})-\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}\big(i(x)\big)\leq\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{L}}c^{{}^{{}_{F}}}(x,x^{\prime})+\frac{2\tau R}{1-\varepsilon_{L}},

where
R\coloneqq\sup_{x^{\prime\prime}\in K_{\mathbb{X}}}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{F}}}\big(i(x^{\prime\prime})\big)
,
K\subseteq\mathscr{P}^{{}^{{}_{F}}}
is a chosen compact such that
4
for
c^{{}^{{}_{F}}}
is fulfilled for
K
, and
K_{\mathbb{X}}
denotes the projection of
K
onto
\mathbb{X}
.

Proof.

We start with the first inequality. By taking the infimum, it suffices to show that for all competitors
\gamma\in\Gamma^{{}^{{}_{M}}}\big(i(x),i(x^{\prime})\big)
,
we have that

(B.11)

\frac{1-\varepsilon_{L}}{4(1+\varepsilon_{L})}c^{{}^{{}_{F}}}(x,x^{\prime})\leq\sum_{j=a+1}^{b}\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}\big(\gamma(j)\big)-\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}\big(i(x)\big)=\sum_{j=a+1}^{b-1}\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}\big(\gamma(j)\big),

where
a<b\in\mathbb{Z}
are chosen such that
\dom(\gamma)=[a,b]_{\mathbb{Z}}
; for the second equality above, note that necessarily
\gamma(b)=i(x^{\prime})
. However, (
B.11
) follows by repeatedly applying
Lemma

B.3
, using the triangular inequality on
c^{{}^{{}_{F}}}
and noting that
\gamma_{2}(j)=\gamma_{1}(j+1)
for all
j\in[a,b-1]_{\mathbb{Z}}
and that
\gamma_{1}(a+1)=x
,
\gamma_{2}(b-1)=x^{\prime}
.

We now turn to the second inequality in (
B.10
), which is a little more intricate to prove. We start by picking some
\varepsilon>0
and a curve
\gamma\in\Gamma^{{}^{{}_{F}}}\big(i(x),i(x^{\prime})\big)
such that

\int_{a}^{b}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{F}}}\big(\gamma(s)\big)\,\mathrm{d}s\leq c^{{}^{{}_{F}}}(x,x^{\prime})+\varepsilon
,
where
a<b\in\mathbb{R}
are such that
\dom(\gamma)=[a,b]_{\mathbb{R}}
, and such that
\im(\gamma)\subseteq K
—
4
guarantees that we can indeed pick such a curve. In the rest of this proof, we will choose appriate control points along the curve
\gamma
to repeatedly apply
Lemma

B.2
to the segments of
\gamma
.

Recalling the indexing conventions on
\gamma
introduced in
Proposition

4.4
, we pick some arbitrary
\varepsilon^{\prime}>0
and iteratively choose controlpoints

a=t_{1}<\dots<t_{n}\in[a,b]
such that—setting
y_{i}\coloneqq\gamma_{x}(t_{i})
—
d(y_{i},y_{i+1})=\max\{\tau|\partial E|(y_{i}),\varepsilon^{\prime}\}
for
1\leq i<n
and such that
d(y_{n},x^{\prime})\leq\tau|\partial E|(y_{n})
. Denoting the length of
\gamma_{x}
by
l
, we have that
n\leq\frac{l}{\varepsilon^{\prime}}
. For each
1\leq i<n
, if

\tau|\partial E|(y_{i})\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}
, we can use
Lemma

B.1
to see that

\displaystyle(1-\varepsilon_{L})\,\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}\big((y_{i},y_{i+1})\big)

\displaystyle\leq\tau\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{F}}}\big(i(y_{i})\big)+\frac{1-\varepsilon_{L}}{2\tau}\varepsilon^{\prime 2}=\frac{1}{2\tau}\big(\tau|\partial E|(y_{i})\big)^{2}+\frac{1-\varepsilon_{L}}{2\tau}\varepsilon^{\prime 2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2\tau}\varepsilon^{\prime 2}+\frac{1}{2\tau}\varepsilon^{\prime 2}=\frac{1}{\tau}\varepsilon^{\prime 2}.

On the other hand, if
\tau|\partial E|(y_{i})\geq\varepsilon^{\prime}
, we can use
Lemma

B.2
to see that
(1-\varepsilon_{L})\,\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}\big((y_{i},y_{i+1})\big)\leq 2c^{{}^{{}_{F}}}(y_{i},y_{i+1})
. Thus, either way,

(1-\varepsilon_{L})\,\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}\big((y_{i},y_{i+1})\big)\leq 2c^{{}^{{}_{F}}}(y_{i},y_{i+1})+\frac{1}{\tau}\varepsilon^{\prime 2}\leq 2\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{F}}}\big(\gamma(s)\big)\,\mathrm{d}s+\frac{1}{\tau}\varepsilon^{\prime 2};

furthermore, also using
Lemma

B.2
, we also have that

(1-\varepsilon_{L})\,\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}\big((y_{n},x^{\prime})\big)\leq 2c^{{}^{{}_{F}}}(x,x^{\prime})+2\tau\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{F}}}\big(i(x)\big)\leq 2\int_{t_{n}}^{b}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{F}}}\big(\gamma(s)\big)\,\mathrm{d}s+2\tau\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{F}}}\big(i(x)\big).

Using the curve
\big(i(x),(y_{1},y_{2}),(y_{2},y_{3}),\dots,(y_{n-1},y_{n}),(y_{n},x^{\prime}),i(x^{\prime})\big)\in\Gamma^{{}^{{}_{M}}}\big(i(x),i(x^{\prime})\big)
as a competitor for the infimum in
\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(x,x^{\prime})
, we finally get

\displaystyle\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}c^{{}^{{}_{M}}}(x,x^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}\big((y_{i},y_{i+1})\big)+\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}\big((y_{n},x^{\prime})\big)+\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}\big(i(x^{\prime})\big)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \leq\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon_{L}}\bigg(2\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\int_{t_{i}}^{t_{i+1}}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{F}}}\big(\gamma(s)\big)\,\mathrm{d}s+(n-1)\frac{1}{\tau}\varepsilon^{\prime 2}+&\\
2\int_{t_{n}}^{b}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{F}}}\big(\gamma(s)\big)\,\mathrm{d}s+2\tau\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{F}}}\big(i(x)\big)&\bigg)+\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}\big(i(x^{\prime})\big)\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon_{L}}\bigg(2\int_{a}^{b}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{F}}}\big(\gamma(s)\big)\,\mathrm{d}s+\frac{l\varepsilon^{\prime}}{\tau}+2\tau R\bigg)+\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}\big(i(x^{\prime})\big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{1-\varepsilon_{L}}c^{{}^{{}_{F}}}(x,x^{\prime})+\varepsilon+\frac{l\varepsilon^{\prime}}{\tau}+\frac{2\tau R}{1-\varepsilon_{L}}+\mathllap{\raisebox{1.50696pt}{${}^{\tau}$}\!\!}\mathcal{L}^{{}^{{}_{M}}}\big(i(x^{\prime})\big).

As both
\varepsilon>0
and
\varepsilon^{\prime}>0
were arbitrary, we can take the limit
\varepsilon\to 0
and
\varepsilon^{\prime}\to 0
in the above inequality to obtain the thesis.
∎
